# Trend ledger — AI Security Radar

Last updated: 2026-08-20

Stage legend: `seed` (first signal) → `emerging` (multi-source, forming) →
`accelerating` (broad, fast) → `mainstreaming` (standard practice) ; `dormant`
(21+ days quiet) ; `declining` (the field moved on). Confidence: `low | medium | high`.
Trend bar: ≥3 independent sources (different orgs/author groups) + ≥1 concrete artifact.

## Active trends

### [id: agentic-attack-surface-001] Attacks on the LLM-agent stack: prompt-injection→RCE, malicious skills, agent supply chain
alias: agent-attack-surface, agentic-security, mcp-attacks
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-06-23
last_evidence: 2026-08-15
evidence:
  - 2026-07-15 — https://www.ayush.digital/blog/the-memory-heist — Ayush Paul "The Memory Heist": a lethal-trifecta data-exfiltration chain on production claude.ai — pairs the naive memory system (a daily-summary paragraph auto-injected into every conversation + a `conversation_search` retrieval tool) with `web_fetch` to exfiltrate the user's stored memories/PII (full name, employer, security-question answers). The tool's exfiltration-avoidance allowlist (a URL must be named in the user message, returned by a `web_search`, or linked inside a prior `web_fetch` result) is DEFEATED by chaining those three criteria to reach an attacker-controlled URL that encodes the stolen data — no code execution or niche MCP needed. A concrete in-the-wild-realistic indirect-PI→exfiltration primitive on a SHIPPING consumer AI agent (distinct from the poisoning/injection facets: the impact is data theft via the model's own tools). Promoted from the queue where it was the standing fresh rotate-candidate.
  - 2026-07-15 — https://www.oasis.security/blog/claude-desktop-vulnerability — "PromptFiction" (Oasis Security, Elad Luz): a zero-click Claude Desktop vulnerability — a crafted `claude://` URL-scheme deeplink hands the agent a full instruction set with NO send-button confirmation and no chance for the user to read it first, ranging (by configuration) from silent conversation exfiltration to code execution; reported to and fixed by Anthropic. Adds a URL-scheme/deeplink zero-click ENTRY-POINT facet to 001's indirect-PI/confused-deputy axis (distinct from the memory-exfil and GitHub-comment facets already on evidence). Promoted from the queue where it was the standing NEXT-cap-slot rotate-candidate flagged since W29.
  - 2026-07-16 — https://oddguan.com/blog/comment-and-control-prompt-injection-credential-theft-claude-code-gemini-cli-github-copilot — "Comment and Control" (Aonan Guan w/ Johns Hopkins) + CVE-2026-47751: the FIRST cross-vendor demonstration that GitHub-comment prompt injection (PR titles, issue bodies/comments) hijacks THREE production GitHub-Actions coding agents (Anthropic Claude Code Security Review, Google Gemini CLI Action, GitHub Copilot Agent) into exfiltrating the host repo's own Actions secrets, using GitHub itself as the C2 channel (pub 2026-04-15; coordinated disclosure across Anthropic/Google/GitHub). Now CVE-BACKED: NVD published CVE-2026-47751 (Anthropic Claude Code Action <1.0.74, 2026-07-16) — the action checked out attacker-controlled PR HEAD branches and read `.mcp.json` via default settings + `enableAllProjectMcpServers`, so a malicious PR `.mcp.json` achieved runner RCE plus workflow-secret exfiltration; fixed by restoring `.claude/`+`.mcp.json` from the PR BASE branch. A 2nd independent researcher (John Stawinski IV, "Trusting Claude With a Knife") corroborates the same GitHub-Actions-coding-agent PI surface. Promoted from the queue where it sat as the standing top rotate-candidate since 07-13 (now vendor-fixed, not just a disclosure blog).
  - 2026-08-03 — https://embracethered.com/blog/posts/2026/hijacking-litellm-for-fun-and-profit/ — Johann Rehberger (Embrace The Red) "LLM Heist: Hijacking LiteLLM for Traffic Interception, Key Theft, and Tool-Call Injection": a set of red-team TTPs against LiteLLM, a widely-deployed AI GATEWAY that holds the backend LLM-provider keys — compromise of the gateway enables provider-key theft, interception/rerouting of LLM traffic, response modification, and TOOL-CALL INJECTION into downstream agent workflows. A concrete attack on the LLM-proxy/gateway layer of the agent supply chain, distinct from 001's skill/MCP-marketplace, memory and indirect-PI facets (this targets the shared gateway every downstream agent trusts), from a top independent researcher whose blog is itself the primary. Rotated in this session; rotated out Agent SkillSlip (03-08, oldest evidence, reducing oddguan.com over-representation 3→2 and refreshing 001 toward the under-covered gateway/proxy layer).
  - 2026-08-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15108 — "Beyond Direct Access: Resource Hijacking in LLM Agents" (ResourceHijackBench): the first systematic study of agent RESOURCE HIJACKING — inducing an agent to invoke/consume/transfer/control the high-value resources it can already reach (compute infrastructure, credentials, usage budgets, identities, private knowledge, communication channels, org workflows) for the attacker's goals WITHOUT directly obtaining the resource or its credentials, a target class prior agent-security work (which studies instruction/data/tool-behavior attacks) overlooked. Six resource categories, 300 scenarios / 900 attack prompts run in isolated envs that record ACTUAL resource use (so attacks are graded on behavior, not text): OpenClaw 84.06% avg ASR, 69.98–89.58% across backends, and the strongest evaluated defense still leaves 55.11% ASR — a new agent attack surface distinct from 001's memory/skill/gateway facets (the resource itself is the target), touching but broader than 012's budget-consumption angle.
  - 2026-07-20 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17535 — "Salience Induction against Multi-Hop RAG Agents": identifies a THIRD attack surface on agentic RAG distinct from content-poisoning (false facts) and prompt-injection (embedded directives) — the SALIENCE CHANNEL: fact position, emphasis, framing and semantic proximity redirect multi-hop attribute-binding even when every retrieved claim is TRUE and NO instruction is present. Formalizes Salience Induction as truth-preserving edits that leave the retrieval trace semantically intact, defines six salience-editing operator classes with an iterative proposer-verifier pipeline under factual+stealth constraints, and ships SalientWiki-MH; effective across five frontier families (GPT/Claude/Gemini/DeepSeek/Qwen) and three agent architectures (ReAct/Reflexion/tool-calling). A non-instruction, non-false-fact agent-reasoning-redirection facet no other 001 evidence carries (distinct from ADI's data-injection and GhostWriter's memory-store poisoning). Promoted from the queue where it was staged 07-21 as a rotate-candidate.
  - 2026-06-30 — https://adversa.ai/blog/opensource-ai-coding-agents-shell-injection-vulnerability/ — Adversa AI "GuardFall": a systematic survey of eleven popular open-source coding/computer-use agents (opencode, Goose, Cline, Roo-Code, Continue, Aider, Plandex, Open-Interpreter, OpenHands, SWE-agent, plus the triggering NousResearch/hermes-agent — ~548K combined GitHub stars) finds TEN of eleven leave the agent-to-bash execution boundary exploitable via decades-old shell bypasses (quote removal, `$IFS`, command substitution, base64-to-sh, destructive argv flags) that defeat regex/tokenized command guards — bash's lexer runs AFTER the pattern check, so any string that is bash-equivalent to a blocked pattern slips through; live end-to-end exploitation confirmed against production binaries driven by a real frontier model (Claude Sonnet 4.6) in a sandboxed `$HOME`. Only Continue implements the guard correctly. A systematic, methodologically rigorous confused-deputy/RCE facet distinct from every other 001 evidence item (none characterize the shell-execution boundary itself across the open-source coding-agent ecosystem). Rotated onto evidence, bumping Unit 42's OpenClaw/ClawHub skills disclosure (2026-06-23) — its "deployed scanners evaded in production" finding is already carried forward by ai-defense-tooling-unreliable-003 (its own axis), so 001 loses no unique coverage; preserved in git/reports.
  - 2026-07-20 — https://www.island.io/blog/agentbaiting-how-800-fake-ai-skills-and-mcp-servers-delivered-malware — Island Security (Oleg Zaytsev) "AgentBaiting": ~7,600 malicious GitHub repositories from ~6,600 profiles, of which 800+ pose as AI SKILLS or MCP SERVERS (Gmail/WhatsApp/Databricks/Jenkins/Docker integrations), appearing 600+ times across public AI registries/catalogs and delivering SmartLoader->StealC infostealer with 14M+ measured downloads. The genuinely new mechanism is an attack on the agent's DISCOVERY layer: an AI agent searching for a capability finds a campaign repo on its own and treats the attacker's README as legitimate documentation — Claude Code, Gemini and ChatGPT each surfaced and recommended malicious repos in testing WITHOUT ever being shown a link, so the compromise happens BEFORE any code runs. Distinct from every other 001 evidence item (all of which assume the malicious artifact has already been reached) and the in-the-wild skill/MCP-marketplace evidence 001 has lacked since Unit 42's ClawHub line was rotated out 07-28; the standing top rotate-candidate since 08-04, promoted this session. Rotated in over ADI (2607.05120, 07-06) — its data-injection-vs-instruction taxonomy reframe is now the established framing echoed across queued items and given a stronger real-world anchor by AgentBaiting's "attacker content treated as trusted documentation" mechanism; ADI preserved in git/reports. island.io STAGED as a discovered-source candidate (2nd on-axis post verified this session — see Discovered-source candidates).
  - 2026-07-28 — https://enklypesalt.com/posts/context-collapse-part3-ai-worming-through-word — Håkon Måløy "Context Collapse, Part 3 — AI Worming through Word" (updated 07-30): the first public demonstration of a DOCUMENT-BORNE SELF-REPLICATING prompt-injection worm. Hidden instructions in a source document used as material in Copilot for Word are interpreted as part of the user's request AND copied into the Copilot-generated/edited output document, turning that downstream document into a fresh carrier — so the payload propagates through ordinary trusted document workflows without the attacker's original file ever being present again. Part 3 of the author's Context Collapse XPIA series, extending single-interaction cross-domain prompt injection to PROPAGATION across a document supply chain; coordinated disclosure with MSRC and Microsoft product teams over an extended 144-day window (90-day period extended twice), with reproduction steps, videos and exact PoC prompts supplied — and no mitigation covering the full attack class as of publication. A SELF-PROPAGATION facet no other 001 evidence item carries (every current line is a single-shot compromise; the closest prior art is Morris II in GenAI email assistants, which this generalizes to documents). Rotated in over "GhostWriter" (2607.06595, 07-06) — its agent-memory-store poisoning facet is now carried by the fresher, more novel black-box VISUAL memory poisoning of Lucid plus ADI's data-injection reframing already on evidence, with MemPoison/Bad-Memory/Memory-Provenance-Laundering queued; GhostWriter preserved in git/reports.
  - 2026-07-20 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15657 — "Lucid" (Do Agents Dream of False Memories?): the first BLACK-BOX VISUAL memory poisoning/injection attack on multimodal AI agents — imperceptible image perturbations under a strictly image-bounded threat model (no access to the MLLM, retriever, or text channel) reach 61.6% poisoning / 58.4% injection ASR across five black-box memory architectures, including a commercially-deployed one. A NEW multimodal/visual facet of 001's memory-poisoning sub-cluster, distinct from every text/email-channel item already on evidence (GhostWriter, ADI). Promoted from the queue where it sat as the standing top rotate-candidate since 07-21 (12+ days, flagged as a routing-risk at the W31 weekly).

That is an attack on the agent's DISCOVERY layer, before any code runs, distinct from every current 001 evidence item (which all assume the malicious artifact has already been reached); it is also the in-the-wild skill/MCP-marketplace evidence 001 has lacked since Unit 42's ClawHub line was rotated out 07-28. island.io staged as a discovered-source candidate. Other below-cap 001 captures this session (all queued): **"Exposed by Design"** (2608.00150) — the first dynamic behavioral security assessment of internet-facing MCP servers at scale (passive discovery across 11 sources incl. Shodan/Censys/FOFA/Smithery/glama.ai, 640 confirmed production servers, 414 dynamically audited with the purpose-built Corvus framework's 34 test modules over 10 MCP vulnerability classes): 68 reportable vulns (SQLi, cloud-metadata SSRF, prompt-template injection, cursor-manipulation path traversal), 91.8% of audited servers lack OAuth, 687 tool instances expose shell execution with no access control, and 41.6% of confirmed servers vanish within three days between runs — deployment outpacing review; ships Corvus as open source. **EvoBreak** (2608.01759) — an experience-conditioned sequential attack on SELF-EVOLVING agents in which every individual attack-stage task is benign, and only the COMPOSITION of the distilled experiences weakens the safety boundary (no direct memory access, no explicitly malicious record), with the BreakGym synthesis pipeline. **Adversarial Attacks in Multi-Agent LLM Pipelines** (2608.00718) — argues agentic pipeline vulnerability is an ARCHITECTURAL property, not a model property: attack success tracks pipeline structure rather than model capability across GPT-5-mini/Claude Sonnet 4.5/Kimi K2.5, rooted in a missing "boundary verification" primitive at inter-agent handoffs; demonstrated on annotated GAIA and SWE-Bench production traces. **From Monoliths to Swarms** (2608.00202) — a taxonomy of attack vectors specific to multi-agent WEB systems plus the WebMASLab test-bed, holding task/tools/browser fixed to isolate the architectural effect of single- vs multi-agent decomposition. **Embrace The Red "LLM Heist: Hijacking LiteLLM"** (08-03) — red-team TTPs against the AI GATEWAY layer (LiteLLM holds the backend provider keys): traffic interception, provider-key theft, response forgery + injected TOOL-CALLS into responses reaching AI clients, and live-chat collection for model distillation — a gateway/control-plane facet touching 001 and 006. **veganmosfet "From /Init to Code Execution with Opus 5"** (07-27, HN pointer, primary NOT opened — unverified). NEW below-cap 001 captures this session (all queued, 001 at cap): SkillJack (2608.03509) — the first attack on the experience->skill pipeline of self-evolving agents: poisoned experiences are transformed by the agent itself into durable malicious skills (sanitization-whitewashing drops safety detection 98.5%->11.4%, 56-89% ASR, 80% persist after deleting the source records), a provenance/skill-lifecycle attack surface distinct from memory/retrieval poisoning (pairs with the queued EvoBreak on the self-evolving-agent nucleus; code on Tencent AI-Infra-Guard); AntiSkillBench / "When Agents Learn to Be You" (2608.03700) — persona-skill privacy leakage + behavioral impersonation across three frontier agents, defenses distillation-dependent and weak; PRWeaver (2608.02693) — LLM PR-auditor evasion when benign+malicious diffs co-occupy the auditor's active review context (detection falls to 16-22% under whole-window review), pairs with IssueTrojanBench/Comment-and-Control on the coding-agent-reads-untrusted-content facet; and a NEGATIVE result (2608.03169, prespecified equivalence) finding no unauthorized tool use across 840 GPT-5.6 trajectories under varied reasoning effort — an access-control datapoint, noted. ### [id: ai-vuln-discovery-002] LLM/agentic vulnerability discovery, repair & the (in)security of AI-written code

Coordinated disclosure with Microsoft. A concrete deployed-autonomous-exploitation datapoint distinct from 002's benchmark/audit evidence (Mako, KASS, the zkSecurity crypto series) — full end-to-end discovery-to-proven-RCE with zero human intervention, on live production infrastructure of a major vendor. (Secondary press corroborates a 2026-07-27 publish date; the primary page itself carries no visible dateline.)
  - 2026-07-28 — https://www.anthropic.com/research/discovering-cryptographic-weaknesses — Anthropic FRT "Discovering cryptographic weaknesses with Claude": Claude Mythos Preview, operating semi-autonomously with multi-agent collaboration (Python + Sage + published literature, ~$100K API cost per attack, minimal human oversight), found an improved attack against the HAWK post-quantum signature scheme (reduces effective keysize from 2^64 to 2^38) and a "Möbius Bridge" meet-in-the-middle attack on 7-round AES (128-800x faster than prior methods), plus results on 13-round LEA and 6-round Serpent. Names CVE-2026-45445 (OpenSSL) and CVE-2026-5194 (wolfSSL) as concrete downstream artifacts. A capability-anchor case that upgrades 002's "AI vuln discovery" axis into deep cryptanalysis specifically — the same genre as zkSecurity's deployed dual-agent crypto-audit series already on this trend, but from the model vendor itself. Rotated out Anthropic's earlier "exploit-evals" (2026-05-22, oldest evidence line by date; superseded in kind by this more concrete, more recent capability disclosure).
  - 2026-07-30 — https://embracethered.com/blog/posts/2026/pipewire-flatpak-linux-sandbox-escape-cve-2026-5674 — Embrace The Red (Johann Rehberger) "Escaping Linux Sandboxes via PipeWire (CVE-2026-5674)": a Flatpak-to-host sandbox escape found by the author's AUTOMATED RESEARCH PIPELINE running Claude Code with Opus 4.6 (April 2026), then manually reproduced and reported — his first bug submitted to Red Hat. The escape chains three issues starting from PipeWire's PulseAudio compatibility layer never validating the 256-byte authentication cookie it demands (it checks the length, then discards the value), so a "Hello World" Flatpak app holding only the standard `--socket=pulseaudio` audio permission reaches full access to the user's desktop, files and credentials; the same path applies to other sandbox tech (Docker, etc.) that connects a socket to PipeWire. A qualitatively different 002 datapoint from the trend's vendor/lab-lab evidence: AI-driven vulnerability discovery run by an INDIVIDUAL researcher on general systems software (a core Linux desktop daemon, not AI/agent infrastructure or a crypto library), yielding a real assigned CVE in a component shipped by default on Fedora/Ubuntu/Debian. Rotated in over Anthropic's "Measuring LLMs' impact on N-day exploits" (2026-06-08, the oldest evidence line) — same-org and superseded in kind by the more concrete, more recent Anthropic cryptographic-weakness capability disclosure already on evidence; preserved in git/reports.
  - 2026-07-22 — https://blog.zksecurity.xyz/posts/bron-bugs — zkSecurity "AI meets Cryptography 3: What AI Found in Bron Labs's bron-crypto" (Stefanos Chaliasos, Hao Pham, False Witness Team): the 3rd installment of zkao's deployed dual-agent AI-audit pipeline (Claude Opus 4.6 primary + Codex 5.3 independent validator, and vice versa), this time on bron-crypto, Bron Labs's Go library for MPC and threshold signatures — 30 findings total, including a CRITICAL Lindell17-DKG operand-swap bug that corrupts threshold-ECDSA key shares; bounties earned under Bron's bug-bounty program. The series now spans three real, independently-audited targets (CIRCL, OpenVM, bron-crypto) — a consistency signal that deployed dual-agent crypto auditing generalizes across cryptographic-library classes, not a one-off result.
  - 2026-07-14 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12316 — "Antiproof: Synthesizing Vulnerability Detectors and Proofs of Exploitability": an end-to-end AI vuln-discovery system that attacks the recall-vs-validation tradeoff bounding automated discovery — it pairs neuro-symbolic detector SYNTHESIS (learns + iteratively refines static detectors from vuln datasets for high recall) with proof-of-exploitability ORACLES that validate each candidate by verifying an executable proof of concrete attacker capability. Detects 64/66 on BountyBench + a curated KEVBench, improving recall >60 percentage points over static-detector baselines. The trend's first evidence for validated AI vuln discovery WITH self-verification (distinct from Mako's exploitation-coverage and the frontier-lab exploit-development lines); promoted from the queue where it was the standing top rotate-candidate.
  - 2026-07-13 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11288 — "Mako: A Self-Evolving Agentic Operating System (SE-AOS) for Autonomous Web Exploitation": the first SE-AOS instance — an agent that treats exploit capability as a mutable, versioned KERNEL it extends at RUNTIME (observes its own failures, synthesizes new capabilities, proves them against a live target, hot-loads them back into itself), deployed as the autonomous web-exploitation engine of the LaunchSafe platform. On the public XBOW validation benchmarks (104 containerized CTF-style web apps, 26 vuln classes, 3 difficulty tiers) it drives EVERY one of the 104 targets to emit a cryptographically fresh per-build flag under a verification regime — a deployed, full-coverage autonomous-web-exploitation result that pushes the trend's frontier-lab + in-the-wild-crypto-audit evidence toward a self-improving offensive agent.
  - 2026-07-17 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15673 — "Beyond Detection: Agentic Attack Synthesis and Simulation for Smart Contracts" (KASS): a multi-agent framework for EXECUTABLE smart-contract exploit synthesis that goes past detection to verify exploitability end-to-end — decomposes exploit generation into planning/generation/testing with retrieval-augmented planning over real audit knowledge, formal generation+validation constraints binding attack plans to executable PoC tests, and a hierarchical dual-loop refinement (code-level repair + strategy-level replanning when assumptions fail). Generates executable exploits for 94.23% of 104 SmartBugs-Curated contracts across four vuln categories and validates real CVE-tagged contracts, beating REX/AdvSCanner and a reproduced Claude Code baseline. Extends autonomous AI EXPLOITATION (executable, self-validating attacks) to the smart-contract domain, distinct from Mako's web-exploitation coverage and Antiproof's detector-synthesis-plus-PoE. Promoted from the queue where it was staged 07-20 as a rotate-candidate.
  - 2026-08-13 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13404 — "Does Fixing Break Security? An Empirical Study of Security Degradation in Iterative LLM-Driven Infrastructure-as-Code Repair": the (in)security-of-AI-written-code facet — the standard iterative validator-feedback repair loop (Checkov/terraform-validate errors fed back for successive fixes) actively DEGRADES security while fixing other issues. Across 5,968 IaC-Eval timelines / 4,440 iteration transitions (15 configs), a previously-passing CIS Benchmark check regressing after a repair iteration is a measurable, common event that cumulative-best metrics structurally hide — the first per-iteration security-trajectory analysis for AI-repaired infrastructure code.
  - 2026-08-04 — https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/frontier-ai-vulnerability-burst/ — Palo Alto Unit 42 (Xu Zou) "The Frontier AI Vulnerability Burst: Industrializing Autonomous Zero-Day Discovery in Open-Source Software": a first-party enterprise threat-intel analysis documenting that AI now compresses months/years of manual code review into days and collapses discovery->PoC->validation into a single autonomous-agent workflow (read code, test a hypothesis, generate a working PoC and validate the result in one loop), industrializing zero-day discovery in OSS and narrowing the discovery->patch->deployment window defenders rely on. A distinct vendor-IR group (Unit 42) anchoring 002's autonomous-vuln-discovery axis with an at-scale in-the-wild framing to complement the concrete per-artifact discoveries already on evidence (zkao/CIRCL/OpenVM, Antaeus, Mako, PipeWire-CVE). CLEAN APPEND (002 was at 9), last_evidence 07-30->08-04.
  - 2026-08-14 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14533 — "Finding Vulnerabilities via LLM-Augmented Semantics-Aware Type-Checking" (SETYPE/PYSETYPE): a semantics-aware type system derived directly from source code from the natural-language MEANINGS of symbols and expressions, where both type inference and checking are performed by LLMs and a FAILED type check flags a potential vulnerability — a discovery method orthogonal to hand-encoded syntactic static-analysis rules. PYSETYPE reaches 87% detection precision / 88% accuracy on real Python web applications and surfaced 15 potential zero-days, NINE confirmed by developers. A distinct 002 datapoint — real, developer-confirmed zero-days from an LLM-as-type-checker approach (not an autonomous-exploitation agent or a crypto-audit pipeline), extending AI vuln DISCOVERY to semantic type-level reasoning. ### [id: ai-defense-tooling-unreliable-003] The AI-security tooling layer itself is unreliable/attackable: skill scanners, prompt-injection detectors & jailbreak judges fail under attack
alias: ai-security-tooling-failure, guard-reliability, eval-reliability
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-06-25
last_evidence: 2026-08-17
evidence:
  - 2026-07-30 — https://adversa.ai/blog/agent-skill-scanners-bypass-eight-tested/ — Adversa AI "A hole in every one": ran eight open-source AI skill scanners (Cisco skill-scanner, NVIDIA SkillSpector, mondoo skillcheck, skillcop, claude-skill-antivirus, huifer skill-security-scan, ai-skill-scanner, hackmyagent) against real malicious-skill attacks — a single universal bypass (encoding: base64/hex/ROT13, plus Unicode-normalization homoglyphs) defeated EVERY scanner, including hackmyagent, the current #1 on the Open Agent Security Benchmark (OASB) leaderboard (F1 0.829) — which ALSO produces 1,753 false positives (FPR 0.438) on benign skills, showing leaderboard metrics don't predict real-world performance. Root cause: "every scanner matches the bytes in the file, not the bytes that execute" — none decodes an arbitrary blob and re-runs its ruleset on the plaintext. A 4th, empirically broader guard-defeat (after SkillCloak/skill-scanners, ShadowPickle/model-file-scanners, ALIBI/vuln-detectors) that reconfirms and quantifies the trend's founding claim with 8 concrete scanner defeats instead of 1. Rotated out Trail of Bits's "The Sorry State of Skill Distribution" (2026-06-03, oldest evidence line by date — the conceptual precursor to this same finding, now superseded by harder empirical data on the identical claim).
  - 2026-08-13 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12880 — "Labels Are Not Endpoints: Treatment Leakage and Construct Validity in MCP Agent Security Evaluation": the eval-reliability facet turned on a security benchmark's own scoring — an audit of a preserved MCP-agent attack campaign (10,200 execution rows → 180 model-bound requests) finds the historical grader had direct TREATMENT LEAKAGE (treatment metadata, not behavior, gated the ATTACK_SUCCESS class), so identical behavior could flip class under relabeling. A treatment-blind reconstruction corrects 58 false ATTACK_SUCCESS / HIJACK_ATTEMPT labels to authorized-benign completions (locked v2 census: zero ATTACK_SUCCESS), while preserving the three genuine protected-data transfers — construct-invalid graders silently manufacture the attack-success rates the field cites.
  - 2026-06-24 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25487 — "How Reliable Is Your Jailbreak Judge?": the automated ASR judges nearly every jailbreak/prompt-injection paper relies on are rarely checked; against 596 human-labeled HarmBench completions the dedicated classifier over-flags (recall 0.974, precision 0.835) while LLM-as-judge recall is erratic (0.06–0.65) and the judges are themselves attackable — the field's measurement layer is unreliable.
  - 2026-08-03 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02820 — "Evading Chain-of-Thought Monitoring Through Model Poisoning": CoT monitoring — an increasingly load-bearing component of AI-safety stacks — relies on the reasoning trace being informative about the model's actions; this implants CoT-Hidden backdoors (via simple fine-tuning recipes, plus a curriculum-training fallback when direct poisoning fails) that elicit attacker-chosen behavior while the CoT trace stays entirely benign, across reasoning-model architectures and sizes. Reframes CoT monitoring as a trace/response CONSISTENCY problem, not anomaly detection: a deployed safety-monitoring tool defeated by design. Rotated in this session; rotated out "Inherited Circuits, Learned Semantics" (2606.27091, 06-25, the most niche mechanistic-interp item).
  - 2026-07-06 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06596 — "Calibration-Family Overfit": trusted-monitoring — the central AI-control defense where a cheaper trusted model scores an untrusted model's actions for sabotage — is shown to overfit to the untrusted model's LINEAGE, so a monitor's reported accuracy is partly a property of the pairing, not the monitor. Making the untrusted policy family the controlled axis, cross-family transfer collapses: at a 1% audit budget an off-lineage monitor catches 19% of attack attempts vs 41% for an in-lineage monitor (+21.7-pt gap, +0.172 own-family AUROC interaction, leak-free across an 8-family matrix / 4 frontier vendors); the gap is geometric (each lineage's sabotage occupies a different scoring direction). Single-pairing accuracy overstates the safety a monitor delivers against a lineage it was not calibrated on. A 6th independent group on the trend's theme, extending "the measurement/detection layer is unreliable under distribution shift" from PI detectors / jailbreak judges / security classifiers to AI-control SABOTAGE MONITORS.
  - 2026-07-13 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11086 — "Rethinking MCP Security: A Large-Scale Study of Runtime MCP Servers and Security Scanner Reliability": builds MCPZoo (the largest dynamic-analysis collection of MCP servers to date, via a multi-agent framework that turns in-the-wild static repos into running services) precisely because MCP-security assessments so far rely on scanners applied to a handful of cases whose RELIABILITY is unverified — and re-measures how MCP security is actually detected at runtime scale. A 7th independent group on the trend's theme, extending "the detection/measurement layer is unreliable" from PI detectors / jailbreak judges / security classifiers / AI-control sabotage monitors to the MCP-security SCANNERS the agent ecosystem now leans on.
  - 2026-07-16 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13075 — "The Entanglement Wall: Activation-Space Probes as Risk Detectors, Not Context Adjudicators": tests whether residual-stream activation probes — an increasingly-deployed guard class — distinguish harmful requests from surface-matched benign controls. Across three 7-8B model families an activation sensor blocks 95.5-97.7% of judge-confirmed compliant attacks BUT also blocks 59.6-68.4% of benign XSTest prompts, and while a disjoint audit reconstructs near-ceiling source-contrast AUROC (0.996-0.999), fixed transfer to matched pairs collapses to 0.590-0.690 — no evaluated axis reaches the specified threshold without direct pair-boundary fitting. An 8th independent group extending "the detection layer looks strong on-benchmark yet fails under context shift" to a NEW detector class (activation-space/residual-stream probes), whose reported accuracy is a property of the source-contrast split, not a deployable operating point.
  - 2026-08-17 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16246 — "CompoSkill: Compositional Skill Chain Attacks from Individually Scanner-Passing LLM Agent Skills": marketplace skills are certified one at a time, but skill-composition risk is a PATH-level property, not a node-level one — a skill can pass its per-skill scanner yet form a risky chain when an agent connects its outputs/capabilities/side-effects with other passing skills, which is exactly why per-package scanners intercept only a limited fraction. A dual attacker (white-box injects explicit skill-id sequences; black-box knows only a role profile, downloads top marketplace skills, builds a Skill Composition Graph and searches high-risk chains whose implicit lures never name skill-ids) reaches Chain-Formation-Rates up to 83.3% (white-box) / 80.6% (black-box) on CompoSkill-Bench (1,140 records, OpenClaw+Nanobot), with a bridge-bonus-then-hop-decay pattern past 3 skills — the 2nd independent group (with ColluSkill 2608.09732, queued 08-11) exposing the compositional blind spot of single-skill certification.
  - 2026-07-20 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17503 — "ShadowPickle: Evading Machine Learning Model Scanners via Stealthy Pickle Deserialization Attacks": three stealthy pickle-deserialization attacks that abuse the Pickle VM's external-module import mechanism to execute payloads during model deserialization AND evade state-of-the-art scanners — evaluated against TEN SOTA model scanners and FOUR model hubs (the Overwritten variant reaches a 63% evasion rate), shipping PickleBench to auto-inject the attacks into arbitrary benign pre-trained models. 9th independent group and the SECOND end-to-end guard-DEFEAT on this trend (after SkillCloak): where SkillCloak weaponized agent-skill-scanner evasion, ShadowPickle weaponizes MODEL-FILE-scanner evasion across the HuggingFace-style model supply chain — a distinct scanner class, confirming the active-weaponization cadence the trend's accelerating trigger required. The evasion is against the deployed model-scanning layer the ecosystem now relies on, not a measurement of it.
  - 2026-07-27 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24964 — "ALIBI: Adaptive Agentic Attacks on LLM-Based Vulnerability Detectors via Adversarial Code Comments": a coding AGENT that implements new functionality, deliberately introduces a vulnerability, and strategically inserts adversarial source-code comments to evade an LLM-based vulnerability detector's reasoning — without changing program behavior. A 10th independent group and the THIRD end-to-end guard-DEFEAT on this trend (after SkillCloak's skill-scanner evasion and ShadowPickle's model-file-scanner evasion): ALIBI weaponizes evasion of AI-based VULNERABILITY-DETECTION/code-review tooling specifically, a distinct deployed-guard class from either prior weaponization and squarely on this trend's "AI security tooling fails under attack" theme applied to the code-security layer.

### [id: adversarial-trigger-backdoor-004] Adversarial trigger implantation and backdoor attacks across ML model types
alias: backdoor-attacks, trigger-poisoning, ml-backdoors
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-06-27
last_evidence: 2026-08-11
evidence:
  - 2026-07-28 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25479 — "Architectural Backdoors in Vision-Language Model Supply Chains via Representation Steering": a malicious model-supply-chain provider embeds a DORMANT steering backdoor directly into a VLM's ARCHITECTURE (a trigger-gated additive modification of an intermediate representation) rather than poisoning training data, controlling fine-tuning, or modifying deployment-time prompts — when the trigger is absent the modification is exactly zero (clean utility preserved); when present, a steering direction shifts the internal representation toward an attacker-defined objective, compromising integrity, safety enforcement AND ranking fairness across VQA/text-to-image/retrieval/response-biasing tasks and multiple VLM families. Ships an auditing defense that inspects distributed EXECUTABLE LOGIC, not just learned weights. A 15th independent attack group and the trend's 3rd member on the LLM/VLM sub-cluster (with "When the Aggregator Cheats" federated-LLM + ReShift VLM-reasoning) — meeting the pre-registered confidence-upgrade trigger.
  - 2026-08-11 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10959 — "Once Poisoned, Arbitrarily Controlled: A Programmable Backdoor in VLMs": breaks the static one-to-one / N-to-N assumption of prior VLM backdoors — a SINGLE poisoning phase implants a PROGRAMMABLE backdoor, letting the attacker choose previously-unseen target-caption semantics at INFERENCE time and synthesize a matching stealthy trigger on demand (any-to-any caption control, target selection decoupled from poisoning). Two components: a heuristic poisoning strategy that teaches a general "trigger-as-instruction" rule (not a memorized pattern) + a feature-space trigger-steganography map turning any target caption into a norm-controlled perturbation or non-semantic patch; high any-to-any success, preserved clean utility, survives several classical backdoor defenses. A qualitatively stronger backdoor threat model than every other 004 item (all of which bind triggers to a fixed target set before training). Rotated in this session, refreshing 004 from a 14-day quiet drift; rotated out the oldest/narrowest line, "Color Matters" (2606.25858, 06-24) — its federated-learning coverage is retained by "When the Aggregator Cheats" (2606.27511); preserved in git/reports.
  - 2026-06-24 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26285 — "TEMPO-Diffusion": targeted time-conditioned backdoor on diffusion models (in-distribution multi-class triggers, in-painting) — localizes malicious distribution shift to temporal exposure, evading standard detectability assumptions.
  - 2026-06-25 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27511 — "When the Aggregator Cheats": a malicious federated-learning aggregator implants advertisement-type backdoors into federated LLM QA models WITHOUT any client data — it recovers representative samples from clients' uploaded gradients (5–20% suffices) and uses them to build a trigger-poisoning set, reaching ~100% ASR with negligible clean-task degradation. 5th independent group; extends the cluster from vision/sensor/diffusion to federated LLMs and to a data-free, gradient-leakage-based threat model.
  - 2026-07-01 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00361 — "ReShift": aha-moment-driven REASONING-LEVEL backdoor on Vision-Language Models — instead of manipulating final outputs (which leave inconsistent/detectable reasoning traces), ReShift redirects the internal chain-of-thought trajectory while preserving surface coherence, via a Poisoned Reasoning-Aware Data Construction pipeline + supervised-RL joint optimization, and formalizes "Entropy Rebound" as a signal of reasoning redirection. 6th independent attack group; adds a VLM reasoning-trajectory domain and a stealthier CoT-level trigger to the cluster.
  - 2026-07-02 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01702 — "Pmeta-TLA": a backdoor attack on SPEECH-classification models (voice/intelligent-gadget domain) via a Timbre Leakage Attack — disseminates timbre information at the frame level within deep self-supervised speech features so poisoned samples sound natural to humans AND evade DNN trigger-detectors, with a meta-learning variant (Pmeta) for transferability. 7th independent attack group; extends the cross-model-type backdoor cluster to the speech/audio domain with a detector-evading, perceptually-natural trigger.
  - 2026-07-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09473 — "Triggering Stealthy Feature Map Backdoors via Physical Fault Injection in Embedded Neural Networks": a CROSS-LEVEL attack bridging implementation-level physical faults to algorithm-level backdoor learning — a precise fault-injection method reliably drives targeted register values to chosen states during NN inference, and the fault-induced intermediate perturbations serve as a stealthy FEATURE-MAP-level backdoor trigger (activated only under the physical fault, invisible in the input space). 9th independent attack group; extends the cross-model-type backdoor cluster to the EMBEDDED/HARDWARE-fault-injection domain (a trigger that lives in silicon-fault effects rather than in input data), a domain no prior 004 evidence covered.
  - 2026-07-05 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04146 — "!Imperio, smolVLA": trigger-word DATA-POISONING backdoor on Vision-Language-Action (VLA) robotics models — just 3 poisoned episodes in 320 clean ones silently embed a backdoor that, on the trigger word, drives real-world pick-and-place success to 0.0% (the robot locks into a fixed joint configuration = denial-of-service) while clean-prompt behavior holds at ~50%, so the attack is invisible under normal operation; evaluated on smolVLA on the LeRobot platform and framed against the open-source robotics ecosystem's implicit trust in community-contributed data/checkpoints. 8th independent attack group; extends the cross-model-type backdoor cluster to the embodied/VLA-robotics domain (a physical-world DoS trigger).
  - 2026-07-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09115 — "Event Burst Trigger" (EBT): an AVAILABILITY backdoor on event-based SNN object detection (SpikeYOLO) — poison-only training-data triggers induce temporally concentrated event bursts at inference that inflate phantom-object candidates and blow up NMS post-processing cost, needing no architecture/loss/pipeline change. Adds a new attack OBJECTIVE to the cross-model-type cluster — availability/DoS rather than integrity/misclassification — on the neuromorphic/event-vision domain no prior 004 evidence covered.
  - 2026-07-20 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17550 — "(A)iSpy: Parasitic Trojans for Machine Learning Infrastructure": a fundamentally different threat model from every other 004 item — instead of poisoning training data to implant a trigger, a malicious RUNTIME MODULE parasitizes the ML computation graph itself, observing transient tensor states in flight and executing targeted manipulations that are impossible to reach from a data- or model-weight-level attack, exploiting the implicitly-trusted execution environment surrounding inference. A 14th independent attack group and a genuinely NEW attack surface (ML-runtime/infrastructure supply chain) rather than another domain extension of the data-poisoning-trigger pattern every prior evidence item shares. Promoted from the queue (staged 07-21) to restore cadence — the trend had gone 10 days without evidence while several fresh groups sat below the (full) cap.

CutBackdoor (2607.18126, 07-20, quantum-domain parameter-supply-chain backdoor, a 15th group) stays queued below the now-full cap — a pure domain extension, not chosen for this rotation over (A)iSpy's more novel mechanism. 2026-07-29: +1 evidence via cap ROTATION — "Architectural Backdoors in VLM Supply Chains via Representation Steering" (2607.25479, pub 07-28, today's cs.CR batch): rotated out CLIP-guided Diffusion Backdoor for Sensor-based HAR (2606.22837, 06-22, oldest evidence line) — its sensor/IoT domain is a standalone facet with no cluster of its own, while the new item is both a genuinely distinct threat model (architecture-embedded, not data-poisoned or runtime-parasitic like (A)iSpy) AND — critically — the 3rd member of the LLM/VLM sub-cluster the trend has watched since 07-02 ("When the Aggregator Cheats" federated-LLM 06-25 + ReShift VLM-reasoning-backdoor 07-01 + this VLM-architecture item). **CONFIDENCE PROMOTED medium→high** on the pre-registered trigger set 07-02/07-03 ("promote confidence when a single domain dominates OR the LLM/VLM sub-cluster attracts a 3rd member") — the 3rd-member condition is now met after 27 days of the caveat holding across 14 prior evidence rotations; the cluster (federated-LLM poisoning, VLM CoT-reasoning redirection, VLM architectural steering) is coherent enough to call the LLM/VLM surface the trend's converging center even though the wider 004 evidence set still spans many model types. last_evidence 07-20→07-28. ### [id: refusal-direction-mechanics-005] The mechanistic/representation basis of jailbreaks: refusal & harmfulness as manipulable linear directions
alias: refusal-direction, mechanistic-jailbreak, steering-vector-safety, activation-attacks
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-07-02
last_evidence: 2026-08-06
evidence:
  - 2026-08-06 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05578 — "Detecting Safety Training Modification in Language Models via Activation Analysis" (AMS, Activation-based Model Scanner): detects modifications to safety training by measuring the GEOMETRIC STRUCTURE of safety-relevant concepts in activation space — safety training creates measurable separation between harmful/benign classes, and certain modifications (abliteration, uncensored fine-tunes) collapse or rotate that structure; validated across 14 model configs / 4 architecture families (Llama, Gemma, Qwen, Mistral) / 4 safety-modification categories (LOO-CV threshold accuracy 71%). A 10th independent group extending the linear-refusal/activation-geometry account to a deployable pre-deployment scanner for safety-training tampering — the abliteration-detection lineage of "Has This Checkpoint Been Abliterated?" (2607.01854) generalized to a geometric-collapse signal. Found via this session's targeted 005 dormancy check (see notes). Fresh 08-06 item — resets last_evidence 07-14→08-06.
  - 2026-07-09 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08883 — "Optimizing Against Safety Representations: Activation-Guided Adversarial Suffixes and the Geometry of Refusal": introduces Activation-Guided GCG, replacing GCG's output-based objective with a loss that DIRECTLY targets the model's internal refusal DIRECTION, plus Soft-GCG (a continuous relaxation) — finding that suppressing refusal GLOBALLY across all layers/positions beats targeting any single layer-position site, i.e. safety representations are distributed across the forward pass rather than causally localized. This is the pre-registered outstanding trigger the trend had watched since seeding — a genuine end-to-end steering/ACTIVATION-VECTOR JAILBREAK TOOL that operationalizes the refusal-direction account into an optimization attack (not another analysis/interpretability result); an 11th independent group. Rotated in this session; rotated out the founding "Geometry of Refusal" line (2606.22686, 06-21, oldest — preserved on study_shelf and as the trend's conceptual origin in these notes). Found via this session's targeted 005 dormancy check.
  - 2026-06-26 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28153 — "Robust Harmful Features Under Jailbreak Attacks": mechanistic evidence that jailbreaks do NOT erase safety features but selectively suppress specific early-layer attention heads while mid-layer Safety-Aligned Heads stay active even on successful attacks — reading those persistent activations yields training-free, adversarially-robust harmful-content detection (the representation account cuts both ways: attack and defense).
  - 2026-07-01 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00572 — "HARC: Coupling Harmfulness and Refusal Directions": shows harmfulness and refusal are separable directions in the residual stream at prompt-side positions, and that jailbreaks succeed by SUPPRESSING either the refusal or harmfulness direction before any token is generated (distinct attack classes occupy separable regions of the harmfulness-refusal plane); extends the analysis to response-token positions (the model recognizes harm mid-generation even when it missed it at the prompt) and proposes a coupling fine-tune. 3rd independent group formalizing the direction-suppression account of jailbreaks.
  - 2026-07-02 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01854 — "Has This Checkpoint Been Abliterated?": a pre-deployment, threshold-free audit that detects whether an open-weight checkpoint has had its refusal mechanism STRIPPED (abliteration — the canonical operationalization of refusal-direction removal). Combines two cheap internal signals — a reference-anchored activation refusal-gap and a weight-recovery energy of the base→candidate weight difference (z-sum) — separating 57 public abliterations from 37 benign fine-tunes/merges/instruction-tunes across a 273-checkpoint registry (Qwen, DeepSeek-distilled Qwen, Llama, Gemma) at AUROC 0.95. 4th independent group; moves the linear-refusal-direction account from analysis to a deployable weight+activation audit of in-the-wild uncensoring, and shows runtime output-guards cannot catch it (they score generations, not the artifact).
  - 2026-07-02 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02396 — "Fast Multi-dimensional Refusal Subspaces via RFM-AGOP": refines the single-linear-direction assumption — refusal to harmful queries lives in a MULTI-DIMENSIONAL subspace, not one direction — and makes recovering that subspace cheap: adapting the Recursive Feature Machine (RFM) with a probe-informed initialization identifies the multi-dimensional refusal subspace in SECONDS, including on long-reasoning-trace models (Qwen 3) where prior subspace-extraction methods were prohibitively expensive; the extracted subspace serves both steering (attack) and monitoring (defense). 5th independent group formalizing refusal as a manipulable low-dimensional residual-stream structure — the enabling geometry behind steering-vector jailbreaks — and the first to make its extraction tractable on reasoning models.
  - 2026-07-02 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02714 — "Not All Refusals Are Equal: How Safety Alignment Fails Cybersecurity at Scale": a large-scale abliteration study across 24 open-source LLMs showing domain-specific abliteration (selectively stripping refusal for a chosen domain, here CYBERSECURITY) is achievable with standard methodology even on a 1T-parameter MoE (Kimi K2); builds directly on the multi-dimensional-refusal-subspace account (cf. 2607.02396) and finds refusal is distributed widely ACROSS layers — especially in trillion-parameter MoE architectures — so an attacker can isolate and remove the harmful-refusal component. 6th independent group and the strongest OPERATIONAL-uncensoring result on this axis so far (abliteration at trillion-param scale, targeted by domain to unblock offensive-cyber tasks), moving the trend from mechanism/analysis toward a demonstrated at-scale refusal-removal capability.
  - 2026-07-08 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07903 — "Mechanistic Interpretability of LLM Jailbreaks via Internal Attribution Graphs": diagnoses jailbreaks by constructing and aligning paired internal COMPUTATION GRAPHS (prompt-specific inference as structured causal interactions among latent features) for clean vs. attacked prompts — showing adversarial attacks systematically SUPPRESS safety-relevant components, spawn attack-specific features, and REROUTE computation paths; decomposes computation into invariant/suppressed/emergent structures, identifies recurring vulnerability motifs, and validates them via causal interventions on nodes/paths/subgraphs (targeted interventions also improve robustness). 7th independent group on the mechanistic-basis-of-jailbreaks axis — generalizes the linear refusal/harmfulness-direction account (HARC 2607.00572, Robust-Harmful-Features 2606.28153) to a graph-level causal-circuit picture across multiple open-source LLMs and adversarial/jailbreak benchmarks.
  - 2026-07-14 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14147 — "Breaking Refusal in the First Half: A Mechanistic Study of the Prefill Jailbreak": dissects the one-line prefill attack ("Sure, here is") that strips refusal — the harm REPRESENTATION stays intact (a linear probe reads harm at 0.91-0.98 on the flipped-to-compliant prompts, as high as on refused ones) while behavioral refusal drops to chance across four models / three families (1.5-3.8B and 14B), so refusal is a SHALLOW, response-site computation. Localizes it to an EARLY response window (a dose-matched position control shows the first half of the response suffices to break refusal, the second half is nearly inert), confirmed by three converging causal probes: restoring the harm direction there partially re-engages refusal, and injecting the model's own refuse-state reverses the jailbreak (74% held-out). 8th independent group on this trend's mechanistic axis — extends the linear-direction/suppression account (HARC, Robust-Harmful-Features, Internal-Attribution-Graphs) to the specific prefill-jailbreak mechanism and pins WHERE (early response positions) and HOW (harm intact, refusal computation shallow) it fails.
  - 2026-07-11 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10112 — "Minionese: Comprehensive Benchmark and Mechanistic Study of Multilingual LLM Safety": an 18-language multilingual jailbreak benchmark (4 resource tiers, 4 perturbation types) paired with a GEOMETRIC MECHANISTIC analysis showing low-resource-language jailbreaks succeed by routing harmful content through a geometrically MISALIGNED refusal subspace, with a sharp Tier-2→3 safety-regime transition. 9th independent group on the mechanistic axis — extends the multi-dimensional refusal-subspace account (RFM-AGOP) to the MULTILINGUAL dimension, showing the same geometric-suppression mechanism explains cross-lingual jailbreak susceptibility, not just English adversarial prompting.
notes: Seeded 2026-07-02 from a 3-independent-group queue nucleus the radar has tracked since 06-21/06-26 (the queue explicitly flagged "watch for a 3rd to seed a trend"). Coherent theme on scope axis 1 (steering/activation-vector attacks; jailbreaking): safety alignment is encoded as low-dimensional LINEAR directions (refusal, harmfulness) in the residual stream, and jailbreaks are best understood as SUPPRESSION/manipulation of those directions rather than prompt trickery — which is simultaneously the enabling mechanism for steering-vector/activation attacks (22686) AND the basis for training-free robust detection (28153, HARC response-side). Two of three artifacts are defense-flavored, but this trend tracks the MECHANISTIC UNDERSTANDING of jailbreaks (not attack-only), so attack/defense mixing is scope-correct (unlike 004, whose bar is attack groups). ### [id: model-extraction-fingerprinting-006] Model extraction, capability distillation & fingerprinting under restrictive APIs
alias: model-extraction, distillation-attacks, model-fingerprinting
stage: emerging
confidence: medium
first_observed: 2026-07-04

evidence:
  - 2026-08-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09867 — "Stealing Reasoning Traces from Proprietary LLM APIs": leading providers now conceal chain-of-thought by returning it to the client as ENCRYPTED blocks passed back with each subsequent request; these encrypted blocks are fully interchangeable across sessions, users and models within a provider's ecosystem, and injecting a foreign encrypted reasoning trace yields a scalable DECRYPTION jailbreak that recovers the concealed reasoning. An 11th independent group and a genuinely NEW extraction TARGET (the provider-concealed CoT/reasoning trace itself) plus CHANNEL (cross-session encrypted-block reuse), distinct from 006's weights/architecture/embedding/timing/harness/prompt targets. Rotated in this session; rotated out "Embedding Inference Attack" (2607.01276, 07-01) — the oldest/narrowest line, a black-box which-embedding-model fingerprinting precursor now superseded by One-Token's broader fingerprinting.
  - 2026-07-31 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29378 — "PTP: Previous-Token Prediction based LLM Inversion for Near-Exact Prompt Reconstruction": a NEW LLM-inversion technique reconstructing the ORIGINAL PROMPT near-exactly from observed model outputs — unlike prior LLM-inversion work, it does not require fine-tuning a sequence-to-sequence model on large external datasets nor access to the target's weights or logits, working from black-box generations alone. A genuinely distinct extraction TARGET (the input prompt itself, not weights/architecture/embeddings/harness) and technique (previous-token-prediction based reconstruction) from every other 006 evidence item — an 8th independent group on the extraction/fingerprinting core. Found via today's cs.LG explore-supplement, clean append (006 under cap at 7/10).
  - 2026-07-31 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28884 — "Hollow-LLM Attack: Computationally Trivial Weights in Zero-Knowledge Verification of LLM Inference": shows that zero-knowledge (ZK) LLM-inference verification — meant to prove a remote provider actually served the advertised model/weights rather than a tampered or downsized variant — can be DEFEATED with computationally-trivial "hollow" weights that still satisfy the ZK proof's equational-correctness check. A direct attack on the opaque-serving-chain TRUST layer 006 already tracks via One-Token fingerprinting and IRIS's substitution/dilution auditing, but from the opposite direction (breaking a cryptographic verification guarantee rather than statistically detecting a swap) — a 9th independent group, clean append.
  - 2026-07-25 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29144 — "Have I Seen You? Embedding Behavior Signals Synthetic Face Dataset Membership": a dataset-level membership-inference attack that first identifies WHICH synthetic face dataset trained a given face-recognition model, then infers the REAL dataset used to train the generator behind it — showing synthetic biometric data, deployed specifically to reduce privacy exposure, can still leak its real-data lineage two hops removed. Extends 006's membership-inference facet (Cross-Modal MIA) into the biometric/generative-pipeline domain — a 10th independent group, clean append (006 now at cap).
  - 2026-07-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28147 — "Agent Harness Distillation": formalizes INFERENCE-TIME HARNESS EXTRACTION as a new IP-theft target in autonomous multi-agent systems (AMAS, e.g. Hermes) — unlike prior static multi-agent-system IP leakage, an AMAS's coordination harness (the iteratively-optimized reasoning/action orchestration co-evolved with the underlying LLM) emerges dynamically at inference time, and this paper shows it can be extracted/exploited via black-box interaction alone. A 7th independent group and a genuinely NEW extraction TARGET (the agent harness itself, distinct from 006's existing model-weights/architecture/embedding/fingerprint targets) — found via today's cs.CR sweep, clean append (006 was under cap at 6/10).
  - 2026-02-23 — https://www.anthropic.com/news/detecting-and-preventing-distillation-attacks — Anthropic: three industrial-scale in-the-wild distillation campaigns (DeepSeek 150k+, Moonshot 3.4M+, MiniMax 13M+ exchanges) illicitly extracting Claude's reasoning/tool-use/coding capabilities through ~24,000 fraudulent accounts and "hydra cluster" proxy networks; attributed with high confidence via IP/metadata/infra correlation. First vendor-published in-the-wild confirmation of large-scale frontier-model capability extraction via distillation, with concrete detection/response countermeasures.
  - 2026-07-01 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01313 — "NightVision": model-extraction attack recovering LLM architectural parameters even under today's restrictive single-logit APIs (providers already restricted top-k logits/logit-bias in response to prior extraction work; several architectural params remain recoverable).
  - 2026-07-11 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10252 — "One Token Is Enough: Fingerprinting and Verifying LLMs from Single-Token Output Distributions": defines a behavioral fingerprint as the empirical distribution of a model's answers to trivial one-word prompts ("name a random number between 1 and 100"), collected across four languages at a cost of ONE output token per query — and measuring 165 models served via a large commercial aggregator (OpenRouter) shows this weak signal suffices to identify/verify WHICH model actually answers, exposing that a substantial fraction of opaque serving chains (aggregators/resellers/inference providers) deviate from the vendor's reference weights. 4th independent group on the extraction/fingerprinting CORE specifically (the pre-registered promotion trigger) and the cheapest model-identity-verification method yet, targeting the opaque-serving-chain trust gap (existing methods need long texts, token log-probs, adversarial prompts, or owner cooperation).
  - 2026-07-05 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04339 — "One Framework for All: Cross-Modal Membership Inference for Generative Models": first unified membership-inference-attack (MIA) framework applying across text-to-text, text-to-image and image-to-text generative models (prior MIAs treated each modality in isolation and were not cross-applicable), grounded in the modality-agnostic observation that a generative model's output distribution approximates its training distribution.
  - 2026-07-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20723 — "Leaky Language Models (LeakyLMs)": the first demonstration that proprietary model, architecture and deployment details can be stolen from PRODUCTION LLMs using ONLY per-token generation TIMING over a remote API. Two attack families: (1) inference-optimization/deployment leakage — detects whether a provider uses speculative decoding and identifies the draft model's context length (measures Google Gemini Flash 2.5 as using speculative decoding with a ~128K-token draft window); (2) architecture recovery — infers the number of transformer layers and the hidden-dimension size. A NEW model-extraction CHANNEL (a timing side-channel) distinct from every current 006 evidence item — NightVision's single-logit architectural-parameter recovery, Embedding-Inference's retrieved-set model-ID, One-Token single-token fingerprinting, and Cross-Modal MIA — extending extraction-under-restrictive-APIs to a purely timing-based remote attack, and a 6th independent group on the extraction/fingerprinting core.

Two independent arXiv groups (NightVision arch-param recovery; Embedding Inference black-box model-ID) were already queued as a 2-group nucleus; opened and verified Anthropic's own Feb-2026 primary disclosure "Detecting and preventing distillation attacks" as the 3rd independent group — a vendor confirming in-the-wild, at-scale capability extraction from a frontier model by three named labs.
